Let's define a coordinate update for current peak:
```
K = indices where b >= b_max*(1 - delta) (or b >= b_max - eps)
h = zeros(n)
for k in K:
    lo = max(0, k-(n-1)); hi = min(n-1, k)
    # a[lo:hi+1] contributes to h[k-hi : k-lo+1] reversed? For j=lo..hi, i=k-j => range k-hi .. k-lo.
    h[k-hi:k-lo+1] += a[lo:hi+1][::-1]
# normalize h? Update a *= exp(-eta * h / max_h)
```
Check: h_i = ∑_{j: i+j∈K} a_j. For fixed k, j in [lo,hi]; i=k-j; as j increases, i decreases. So h slice reversed.
Then exponentiate. Maybe use `a *= np.exp(-eta * h / hmax)`.
Normalize and clip.

This is exactly subgradient with active set all near peaks. It can reduce peaks because every a_i involved in active diagonals is decreased. It might push mass to positions not in active diagonals. This resembles "peak canceling" and may converge to a low-peak sequence.

4. Active peak update:
```
def active_subgrad_update(a, top=20, mode='frac', frac=0.8, alpha=0.2):
    n=len(a)
    b=fft_conv(a)
    m=b.max()
    if m<=0: return a,b
    # threshold: b >= max(1e-12, m*frac, m - 1e-6)
    if mode == 'top':
       idx=np.sort(np.argpartition(b, -top)[-top:])
    else:
       idx=np.where(b >= m*frac)[0]
       if len(idx)==0: idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(b))])
       # to avoid too many, limit to top 100 by value
       if len(idx)>100: idx=idx[np.argsort(-b[idx])[:100]]
    h=np.zeros(n)
    for k in idx:
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
       if lo>hi: continue
       h[k-hi:k-lo+1] += a[lo:hi+1][::-1]
    # hmax
    hm=h.max()
    if hm<=0: return a,b
    # scaled alpha; maybe multiple α candidates
    return a,b,h,hm
```
Then in optimizer:
```
def optimize_active(a, time_limit, top=20, fracs..., alphas...):
   a=a.copy(); a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s
   best=a.copy(); bs=exact_score(best)
   T0=time
   for outer in ...:
      b,m,... update h
      found=False
      for alpha in alphas: # descending? try 0.5,0.25,0.1
          cand=a*np.exp(-alpha*h/hmax)
          cand=np.maximum(cand, floor)
          if cand.sum()<1e-8: continue
          cand/=sum
          sc=exact_score_cand(cand) (can compute via b_new maybe)
          if sc<best_score: accept; break
      if not accept: try smaller/random/add perturb
   return best
```

Maybe use a line search over alpha with score drop. If no drop, try random perturbation.

Detailed active optimizer:
- Input: array a (sum arbitrary), length n, time budget.
- Normalize sum=1.
- Maintain best.
- Hyperparameters schedule:
   - `warmup`: Use smooth dense gradient with P=b^p p from 1 to maybe 100; then hard active top T from large to small.
- For each phase, number of steps based on time.
- Each step compute b, M, score=2nM.
   - If score < best: update.
   - Determine update gradient:
        smooth: p_power maybe `exp(log_b * p_power - max)`. Use `P` normalized. h via FFT cross-correlation (dense). alpha candidates [0.2,0.1,0.05]. Update a = posify(a*exp(-alpha*h/hmax)). Score.
        hard: choose top `T` (maybe 5, 20, 100) and/or threshold `frac=0.95`. h via active loop.
   - Accept if exact M decreases (or score). If no alpha works, try a random perturbation: multiply a by exp(rng.normal(0, sigma)) and renormalize; if score better than current? If not, maybe still keep for exploration? To avoid losing best, maintain current and best separately. For simulated annealing, accept worse with probability based on T? Could help. def cross_corr_pa(a, P): # a length n, P length 2n-1 -> h length n: h_i = sum_j a_j P_{i+j}
    n=a.size
    N=np.fft.next_fast_len(3*n-2)
    # P zero padded
    fa=np.fft.rfft(P, N)
    fr=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], N)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fr, N)[:n+n-1? actually length 3n-2]
    return 2*conv[n-1:n+n-1]
```

- Algorithm `reduce_max(k, L)`:
   1. Start S random (maybe greedy initial).
   2. While time:
      If Cmax ≤ L: success.
      Choose a max sum s with count C>L. Find pairs (i,j) in S with i+j=s (ordered). For improvement, one of i or j should be moved. Pick one y from a max pair.
      Try random x not in S; evaluate if swap y→x reduces Cmax (or increases but maybe). Need compute new max efficiently? We can update counts and compute c.max; O(k) update + O(2n) max. If accept.

We can also phrase as a SAT/packing: For each sum s, at most L pairs; choose S subset size k. Randomized algorithms for B_2 sets: start random, repeatedly fix a conflict sum >L by deleting one element from it; then add new elements. This is like "satisfiable if enough". We can implement:
   - Build random S of size k.
   - While exists conflict sum count>L:
       Let s be a conflict; pick a random y∈S that is in a pair summing to s (i.e., y or s-y).
       Remove y (k decreases).
       Add random x (not conflict? maybe k increases) if feasible for limit? But removal creates slack.
   - Track max k achieved with no conflicts.
This may converge to a B2[L] set of large size. It's like random deletion/addition for bounded occurrence. Since L small, effective.

def search_bounded(n, L, target_k, time, rng):
   # start with random set of size min(n, target_k + 20) maybe
   # While time:
       if c.max() <= L and k >= target_k: success
       if c.max() > L:
           s = index of max
           # get one or few elements y such that y in S and s-y in S (ordered) or s-y==y
           candidates_y = [y for y in S if y != s-y and in[s-y] or y==s-y and count at 2y conflict]
           y = random.choice(candidates_y)
           remove y; k--
       else # no conflicts but k<target_k:
           # add random x that keeps <=L
           # try some randoms; if none, remove a random element to create slack then add x?
           added=False
           for trial in 50:

              if not in[x] and feasible_add(x): add; added=True; break
           if not added:
              # choose y from a sum with high count (close L) to remove, then try add
              y=random selected; remove
   return best
```

Let's outline `optimize_active(a, time_budget, fixed_mask=None, alpha_list, top_listpower_list)`.
Pseudo:
```
def optimize_active(a, time_budget, fixed_mask=None, start_time=None, verbose=True):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); n=len(a)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000); s=a.sum(); if s<1e-12: a[:]=1.0; s=n
    a=a/s
    if fixed_mask is not None: a[~fixed_mask]=0; a/=sum
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_array(a)
    current=a.copy(); score=best_score
    rng=np.random.default_rng(...)
    # Phase varying
    num_iters=0
    T0=time
    # schedule params:
    # We'll do loop; each iteration choose mode based on elapsed:
       first 30%: smooth p_power=2/5/10? top active large
       middle: top 20/50
       last: top 1/2/5, small alphas
    while time < T0+budget:
       # compute b, max
       b = fft_conv(a)
       max_b=b.max()
       score=2*n*max_b
       if score < best_score - 1e-12: update best/current
       # if very close target, maybe refine.
       # generate h depending on mode:
       use_smooth = (iteration < smooth_iters) or rng...
       if use_smooth:
           p_power = ... 2,5,10,50
           P=power_soft(b,p_power)
           h = dense_h(a,P)
       else:
           top = choose from [5,10,20,50,100]
           frac = choose [1.0,0.95,0.9,0.8]
           idx = active indices
           h = active_h(a, idx)
       hm=h.max(); if hm<=0: random perturb
       # alpha candidates depending on mode: [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05] plus random
       accepted=False
       for alpha in candidate:
           cand=a.copy()
           cand *= np.exp(-alpha*h/hm)
           if fixed_mask: cand[~mask]=0
           cand = np.maximum(cand, floor)
           csum=cand.sum()
           if csum<=0: continue
           cand/=csum
           sc=score_array(cand)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < score - 1e-12: # or best
               a=cand; score=sc; accepted=True; break
           # if sc < best_score? accepted? We'll do score current.
       if not accepted:
           # try random perturbation: multiplicative noise
           noise=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
           cand=a*noise
           if fixed_mask: cand[~mask]=0
           cand=np.maximum(cand, floor); cand/=sum
           sc=score_array(cand)
           if sc < best_score: maybe accept; elif sc < score*1.01 and rng.random()<anneal: accept
           if no accept: very small random alpha? or break phase
       # occasionally mix with best if current worsens
       if best_score < score and (score/best_score>1.05 or time): a=best.copy(); score=best_score
       num_iters++
    return best,best_score
```
Need careful: using `score < score` (current) may accept small improvements but can cycle. Better accept if `sc < best_score` or `sc < current_score`. Keep current. If none, perturb. Track effective improvement.

Let's design B2 search with class `B2State` and functions.

B2 functions top-level:
```
class B2State:
    __slots__ = ('n','limit','sup','pos','in','c','k')
    def __init__(...): ...
    def add(self,x): ...
    def remove(self,x): ...
    def feasible_add(self,x): ...
    def support_array(self): return self.sup[:self.k].copy()
    def max_count(self): return int(self.c.max())
    def binary_score(self): ...
```
But methods are nested? No, class top-level. It's okay. However, if we define class with methods, they are not closures? They are methods, but code-stage likely accepts. To be extra safe, we can define lightweight functions with state dict. But class methods avoid many args. I think class is acceptable. The rule specifically: "Make all helper functions top level and have no closures from function nesting. Don't use any lambda functions." A class method is not a top-level function? It's nested in class, but not a closure. To avoid any objection, use only module-level functions, no class. But passing state dict is easy.

Let's implement module-level functions with state dict:
- `b2_state(n, limit)`: returns dict with arrays.
- `b2_add(st,x)`: modifies st.
- `b2_remove(st,x)`.
- `b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit=None)`.
- `b2_score(st)` returns (max_c,k,score).
- `b2_support(st)` returns copy.

Functions top-level. Good.

Implementation of `b2_add`:
```
def b2_add(st, x):
    c=st['c']; sup=st['sup']; k=st['k']; n=st['n']
    sup[k]=x
    st['in'][x]=1
    st['pos'][x]=k
    if k>0:
        idx = sup[:k] + np.int32(x)
        c[idx] += 2
    c[2*x] += 1
    st['k'] = k+1
```
Need `sup[:k] + np.int32(x)` works. `idx` int32.
`b2_remove`:
```
def b2_remove(st, x):
    c=st['c']; sup=st['sup']; pos=st['pos']; inb=st['in']; k=st['k']
    p=pos[x]
    if p<0 or p>=k: return
    last=sup[k-1]
    # remove x from counts using current support (includes x)
    idx=sup[:k] + np.int32(x)
    c[idx] -= 2
    c[2*x] += 1
    # swap
    sup[p]=last
    st['in'][x]=0; st['pos'][x]=-1
    if p != k-1:
        st['pos'][last]=p
    st['k']=k-1
```
If k-1 is x, no need set pos[last]=last? It's already. But after set in[x]=0, if last==x and p==k-1, st['pos'][x] already -1. Good.

`b2_feasible_add`:
```
def b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
    inb=st['in']; c=st['c']; k=st['k']; limit=int(limit)
    if inb[x]: return False
    if limit < 1: return False
    if c[2*x] > limit-1: return False
    if k>0 and limit>=2? For limit=1, need c<=-1 impossible if k>0. Use if limit-2 >=0 else if c[idx] > -1. Actually new count = c+2. Need c <= limit-2. If limit-2 <0, no any c allowed if c>=0, impossible. So:
       idx_arr = sup[:k] + x
       if limit <= 1: return False
       if np.max(c[idx_arr]) > limit-2: return False
    return True
```
For limit=2, need c≤0, so only sums not present. For Sidon B2[2] ordered count 2; okay.

Greedy build:
```
def b2_greedy_random(n, limit, rng, passes=1, max_time=None):
   st=...
   inb = st['in']
   for pass in range(passes):
      order = rng.permutation(n)
      added_any=False
      for x in order:
         if st['k'] >= n: break
         if b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
             b2_add(st,x); added_any=True
      if not added_any and pass>0: break
   return st
```
For n=6121, one pass 6121 feasible checks length k. Might be okay. For multiple limits, okay.

Local increase:
```
def b2_local_increase(st, limit, rng, time_budget, target_k):
    best_k=0; best_support=None
    T0=time; Tlimit=T0+time_budget
    while time<Tlimit:
       k=st['k']
       if k>best_k: best=k; support=st['support_array']
       maxc=st['c'].max()
       # If maxc<=limit and k>=target: success? We can return
       # Try random additions up to add_trials
       added=False
       for _ in range(50):
          x=rng.integers(n)
          if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
             b2_add(st,x); added=True; break
       if not added:
           # perform swap: choose a to remove, likely from a high count sum
           maxc=st['c'].max()
           s=int(np.argmax(st['c']))
           # collect y in support such that (y,s-y) both in support ordered and count at s contributes
           cands=[]
           for y in st['sup'][:k]:
               z=s-y
               if 0<=z<n and st['in'][z]:
                   # Need ensure pair counted; if y==z, it contributes 1 to c[s]; if y!=z, both ordered pairs at s, count includes2. Any is ok.
                   # To not duplicate, include y if y <= z? choose one.
                   if y <= z:
                       cands.append(int(y))
           if cands:
               y=rng.choice(cands)
             else: y=rng.integers(k) -> sup[y]
           b2_remove(st,y)
           # after removal, try add random x several times
           for _ in range(200):
               x=rng.integers(n)
               if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
                   b2_add(st,x); added=True; break
           # if no add, maybe also add? remove was to increase, not reduce. Backtracking: if no add, re-add y? But y removed may lower max, enabling future. Keep removed for now.
    return best_support,best_score
```
This may remove elements and not recover, but supports can grow again.

We can also allow local search for exact score not limit: If max>limit, the feasible_add won't allow additions until max dropped via removals/swap. The local search with remove from max conflict and add should reduce conflicts.

But for score improvement with actual max, we can start with limit much higher? Actually feasible_add uses limit = current target L (which we desire). If current max >L, no additions until max≤L. Removing from max reduces. Good.

Need `best_support`: But state may later improve; save when k>best_k and max<=limit? Or score (2n*max/k^2) lower. We want not just k under limit but actual score. Since limit is max allowed, if current max>limit, score maybe not yet. We can still save if score lower and k large? But if max>limit, binary score may be above target. But could be best. We'll track based on `2n*maxc/k^2` (if k>0). Use if lower than best_score.

We can also run a direct local score minimization with no fixed limit, using random swaps to reduce `2n*max/k^2`:

Let's draft functions for FFT conv:
```
def fft_convolution(a):
    n = a.size
    if n == 1:
       return np.array([a[0]*a[0]], dtype=float)
    # scale to avoid overflow: asum = a.sum(); a = a/asum; but score function normalizes outside? We can assume sum1 in optimize; for safety:
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s != 0:
       a = a / s
    L = 2*n - 1
    N = np.fft.next_fast_len(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)  # small negatives
    # denormalize? If score needs scale-invariant, return normalized b.
    return b
```
But if caller expects unnormalized, can't. In `score_array`, we need score = 2n*max_b where a normalized sum1. So `score_array` normalizes internally and uses normalized b. That's scale invariant. It should not return b.
```
def convolution_normalized(a): normalize and return b
def score_array(a): b=conv_normalized(a); return 2*n*b.max() if sum>=0.01 else inf
```
If values large but sum positive, normalize; if max after normalization >? fine.

But if a has many zeros and one element, normalized b max=1, score=2n large. Good.

`score_array` should handle `a` list? We'll pass numpy.

`dense_gradient(a, P)`:
```
def dense_gradient(a, P):
    n=a.size
    N=np.fft.next_fast_len(3*n-2)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(P, N)
    fr=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], N)
    hc=np.fft.irfft(fa*fr, N)[:2*n-1+n-1? N]
    h=2.0*hc[n-1:2*n-1]
    h=np.maximum(h,0) # numeric
    return h
```
Wait `P` length 2n-1. `a[::-1]` length n. Convolution length (2n-1)+(n-1)=3n-2. Slice length n from n-1 to 2n-1 exclusive. Good.

Can test small n mentally: a=[1,0], P length3 [p0,p1,p2]. r=[0,1]. Conv P*r: c0=p0*0=0, c1=p0*1 + p1*0=p0, c2=p1*1+p2*0=p1, c3=p2. slice n-1=1 to 2: [c1,c2]=[p0,p1]. Desired h0=2(a0P0+a1P1)=2(p0); h1=2(a0P1+a1P2)=2(p1). After multiply2 => correct.

Active gradient:
```
def active_gradient(a, indices):
   n=a.size; h=np.zeros(n)
   for k in indices:
       lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
       if lo<=hi:
           h[k-hi:k-lo+1] += a[lo:hi+1][::-1]
   return 2.0*h
```
Using np slice and reversed view adds. `k-hi` >=0? k<=2n-2, hi>=0. k-lo <= n-1. Good.

Choosing active indices:
```
def choose_active_indices(b, mode, top, frac):
   m=max(b)
   if mode == 'top':
      t = min(top, b.size)
      part = np.argpartition(b, b.size-t)[-t:]
      return part[np.argsort(-b[part])]
   else:
      thr = max(m*frac, m-1e-9, 1e-12)
      idx=np.where(b>=thr)[0]
      if len(idx)==0: idx=np.array([np.argmax(b)])
      if len(idx)>max_active: limit by top
      return idx
```
Use top values to avoid too many. Maybe always combine: threshold top min(max_active, count above frac). `max_active=200`.

Alpha list: Use `[2.0,1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03]`. But exp(-2) may be heavy; still okay. We can try descending until improvement; if not, no update.

Potential objective: Multiplicative update uses h from current b. If h is all zero? Not.

We must ensure floor doesn't change score much. Use `floor = 1e-12` for free. For fixed, outside zero.

Now, random perturbations:
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
   cand = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=n))
   return cand / sum
```
Could also randomly zero out top elements or add random support.

Time scheduling in `optimize_active`:
- Precompute `conv` each iteration. If no improvement for many iterations, change mode (increase/decrease top or p_power) or random restart from best with perturbation.
- We can use an outer loop over "restarts", each with time_chunk. Start from candidate, run internal optimizer for chunk, return best. This may find multiple local minima. We'll implement a function `optimize_active_single(a, time_budget, ...)` that updates current and best, with restart after stall.
- Inside, keep a `stall` count: if no best improvement for e.g. 200 iterations, perturb current strongly or reset to best plus noise. Then continue.

Since no nested functions, helper functions top-level.

Pseudo for `optimize_active`:
```
def optimize_active(a, time_budget, fixed_mask=None, rng=None, seed=0, init_top=20, min_top=1, use_smooth=True):
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(123)
    T0=time.monotonic(); Tlimit=T0+max(0,time_budget-2.0)
    a = normalize(a, fixed_mask, floor=0)
    best = a.copy(); best_score = score_array(a)
    cur = a.copy(); cur_score = best_score
    best_iter=0; last_best_iter=0
    it=0
    # phases: smooth first for 200 iterations or 10% time
    smooth_iters = 200
    top_schedule=[100,50,20,10,5,3,2,1,2,3,5,10]
    while time<Tlimit:
       if it % 50 ==0: print...
       b=convolution_normalized(cur)
       mb=b.max(); cur_score=2*n*mb
       if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best=cur.copy(); best_score=cur_score; last_best_iter=it; best_score=best_score
       # decide mode
       if it < smooth_iters and use_smooth and cur_score > best_score*1.0? maybe:
           p = [2,4,8,16,32,64][min(5,it//30)]
           P=softmax_power(b,p)
           h=dense_gradient(cur,P)
       else:
           # cycle top
           top = top_schedule[min(len-1, it//100)]
           frac = 0.98 if top<5 else 0.9
           idx=choose_active(b, 'both', top, max_active=200, frac=frac)
           h=active_gradient(cur, idx)
       hm=h.max()
       accepted=False
       if hm>0:
           alphas = get_alphas(it) # maybe [1.0,0.5,...]
           for alpha in alphas:
              cand = cur * np.exp(-alpha*h/hm)
              if fixed_mask: cand[~mask]=0
              cand=np.maximum(cand, floor)
              cs=cand.sum()
              if cs<=0: continue
              cand/=cs
              sc=score_array(cand)
              if np.isfinite(sc) and (sc < cur_score - 1e-12 or sc < best_score): # accept if improves over current or best? Use current to avoid small regress; if sc<best but >cur, would jump? Maybe accept if sc < cur_score or (sc < best_score and rng random)
                  # accept if sc < cur_score; else if sc < best_score and (cur_score > best_score*1.02) maybe
                  cur=cand; cur_score=sc; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
           # small random noise
           sigma=0.05 + 0.5*abs(rng.normal())
           cand=cur*np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,n))
           if fixed_mask: cand[~mask]=0
           cand=np.maximum(cand, floor); cs=sum; if cs>0 cand/=cs
           sc=score_array(cand)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score:
              cur=cand; cur_score=sc
           elif np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score and rng.random()<0.5:
              cur=cand; cur_score=sc
           else:
              # reset to best with noise
              if it - last_best_iter > 200:
                 cand=best*np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,n)); floor; normalize
                 cur=cand; cur_score=score_array(cand)
                 last_best_iter=it? maybe don't reset if losing
       it +=1
    # final if cur_score<best update
    return best,best_score,cur,cur_score
```
This may accept only improvements; if local minimum, random perturb can escape but may not improve enough. We can implement simulated annealing:
- Maintain `temperature` decreasing from 0.01 to 1e-4 relative.
- If new score worse than current but within `exp(-(sc-cur)/T)`, accept. This helps.

anneal = max(1e-4, 0.01*(1 - elapsed/time_budget))

```

Now, `normalize_array`:
```
def normalize_array(a, fixed_mask=None, floor=0.0):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).copy()
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
   a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
   if fixed_mask is not None:
      a[~fixed_mask]=0.0
   if floor>0:
      a=np.maximum(a, floor)
   s=a.sum()
   if s <= 1e-12:
      a[:]=1.0
      if fixed_mask is not None: a[~mask]=0; s=mask.sum()
      if s<=0: a[:]=1; s=n
      a/=s
   else: a/=s
   if fixed_mask is not None: a[~mask]=0; a/=a.sum() # floor on all then zero could reintroduce sum? 
   return a
```
If fixed and floor=0, no floor outside. If fixed and floor>0, set inside floor only? We'll handle:
```
if fixed_mask is not None:
   a[~mask]=0
   if floor>0: a[mask]=np.maximum(a[mask], floor)
```

Let's detail B2 local search more. We can try to maximize k for a given limit L using a deletion from random initial large set. This is similar to "Find a B_2[g] subset of a random set". The maximum size maybe limited, but random restarts can find. We can start with k = target (or larger) and repeatedly delete one element from each max conflict until no conflicts. Then try add. This is akin greedy deletion. Let's implement a function that from a random set of size k0 finds a maximal B2[L] subset:
```
def b2_max_subset(n, L, k0, rng):
   st=empty; add k0 random distinct
   while c.max() > L:
      s=argmax c
      # pick a y in conflict with probability proportional to involvement in high counts maybe
      y=random conflict element
      remove y
   # Now try add random feasible until stuck
   return st
```
Then local swaps to recover size. If k0 too high, final k maybe lower. We can choose k0 around target+20. If maximum B2[L] > target, random subset target likely already feasible? If not, deletion ends near max subset of that random set, which could be below max but maybe close. Multiple restarts.

We can also seed initial set not uniformly random but using greedy to reduce conflicts. For each L, first greedy build feasible as above to get a base. Then add random + repair to increase.

Implementation of repair when a random set has conflicts:
- While max>L:
   s = argmax c.
   Find list of elements y in S such that there exists z = s-y in S. To choose a good y to remove, perhaps choose y with many conflicts: For each y in conflict list, compute `load = sum(counts at y+z for other z?)` expensive. Simpler random.
   Remove y.
- After max≤L, attempt add random feasible. If no add after many trials, do a swap: remove a random y that is in one of the highest sums (not necessarily conflict if limit) and then add random feasible. This can change set.

Direct score function:
```
def direct_score(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
   a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0)
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<0.01: return inf
   # For exact with evaluator normalization not needed; score = 2*n*max(np.convolve(a,a))/s²
   if a.size > 20000: # fallback fft normalized
       b=convolution_normalized(a); return 2*n*b.max()
   b=np.convolve(a,a)
   return float(2*a.size*b.max()/(s*s))
```
Using evaluator's clamping: if a has negative, clip. If a huge, conv may overflow? With sum maybe large but values <=1000, n=6121, b max≤1e6*6121? 6e9, sum²≈(6e6)^2? okay? But if all 1000, n=6121, sum=6.121e6, sum²=3.75e13, bmax=1000*6121*1000? 6.121e9; score2. Fine.

For internal optimization, use normalized score to avoid overflow.

Potential issue: `np.convolve` for int support binary with size n=6121, O37M; fine. For random initial maybe direct a few times okay. For many active iterations use FFT.

Now, active optimizer uses `score_array` normalized as `2n*max_b` because sum1. Need direct score for final. But internal uses normalized; if values sum not exactly1 due floor, okay.

Let's write `convolution_normalized` efficiently:
```
def convolution_normalized(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300: return np.zeros(2*a.size-1)
    a = a / s
    n=a.size
    if n <= 1: return np.array([a[0]*a[0]])
    L=2*n-1
    N=np.fft.next_fast_len(L)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:L]
    np.maximum(b,0,out=b)
    # tiny cleanup: b[b<1e-15]=0? not needed
    return b
```
If a contains NaN, replace before sum.

`score_normalized(a)`:
```
b=convolution_normalized(a); return 2.0*a.size*float(b.max())
```

Need in active optimize, repeated score_array calls allocate b; memory okay. We can optimize by using `score_normalized(cand)` that uses FFT; for n=6121 okay.

Potential performance issue with `np.fft.next_fast_len` per call overhead small. Could cache? Top-level variable? Could use `functools.lru_cache`? Closures? No. We can define a simple global dict cache.

```
_NCACHE={}
def get_fft_len(L):
   if L in _NCACHE: return _NCACHE[L]
   N=np.fft.next_fast_len(L); _NCACHE[L]=N; return N
```
No closures.

Now, `optimize_active` must accept `n` and arrays. Let's refine to avoid infinite loops and ensure best returned.

```
def active_optimize(a, time_budget, fixed_mask=None, seed=1, verbose=False):
    if time_budget <=0: return a, score_array(a)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a=normalize_active(a, fixed_mask, floor=0.0)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_array(a)
    cur=a.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    T0=time.monotonic(); Tlimit=T0+max(0,time_budget)-1.0
    it=0; last_best=0
    top_cycle=np.array([100,80,60,40,30,20,15,10,8,6,5,4,3,2,2,3,5,8,10])
    p_cycle = [2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256]
    # maybe smooth only first part if no fixed mask
    while time.monotonic() < Tlimit:
       # if too many no improvement, reset cur to best with noise
       if it and it - last_best > 500:
           cur = best.copy()
           noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.2, size=cur.size))
           cur *= noise
           if fixed_mask is not None: cur[~fixed_mask]=0.0
           cur = np.maximum(cur, 1e-12)
           if fixed_mask is not None: cur[~fixed_mask]=0.0
           s=cur.sum()
           if s>0: cur/=s
           cur_score=score_array(cur)
           last_best=it
       b=convolution_normalized(cur)
       mb=float(b.max())
       cur_score=2.0*cur.size*mb
       if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
           best=cur.copy(); best_score=cur_score; last_best=it
           if verbose: print('active improve', it, best_score)
       # if best_score <= 1.45 maybe reduce alphas? no
       # choose gradient
       use_smooth = (it < 300) or (use_??? and rng.random()<0.2)
       if use_smooth:
           p = p_cycle[min(7, it//50)]
           P=soft_weights(b, p)
           h=dense_gradient(cur, P)
           hm=float(h.max())
           if hm <=0: h=None; hm=0
       else:
           top = int(top_cycle[min(len(top_cycle)-1, it//80)])
           # for very hard, top 1-2; for broader, top up to 150
           idx = choose_active(b, top, 0.95)
           h=active_gradient(cur, idx)
           hm=float(h.max())
           if hm <=0: h=None; hm=0
       accepted=False
       if h is not None and hm>0:
           # candidate alphas: start moderate, smaller if close to best? Use list.
           alphas = [0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02]
           # maybe include 1.0 for hard top small
           if not use_smooth and top <=3: alphas = [1.0, 0.5]+alphas[1:]
           for alpha in alphas:
               cand = cur * np.exp(-alpha * (h / hm))
               if fixed_mask is not None: cand[~fixed_mask]=0.0
               if fixed_mask is None:
                   cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-12)
               s=float(cand.sum())
               if s <=0 or not np.isfinite(s): continue
               cand /= s
               sc=score_array(cand)
               if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
                   cur=cand; cur_score=sc; accepted=True; break
               if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                   # accept if better than best even if worse than cur? maybe if cur stuck
                   cur=cand; cur_score=sc; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
           # random multiplicative noise; sigma maybe 0.1-1.0
           sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.5))
           cand = cur * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=cur.size))
           if fixed_mask is not None: cand[~fixed_mask]=0.0
           if fixed_mask is None:
               cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-12)
           s=float(cand.sum())
           if s>0 and np.isfinite(s):
               cand /= s
               sc=score_array(cand)
               if np.isfinite(sc):
                   anneal = max(1e-4, 0.02*(1.0 - (time.monotonic()-T0)/max(1.0,time_budget)))
                   if sc < cur_score or (sc < cur_score + anneal*max(cur_score,1e-6) and rng.random() < math.exp(-(sc-cur_score)/max(anneal*cur_score,1e-12))):
                       cur=cand; cur_score=sc
       it += 1
       if verbose and it%200==0: print...
    if cur_score < best_score:
       best=cur.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    # final clean
    return best, best_score
```
This uses current_score update at start and after accepted. But after accepted, next loop recomputes. Good.

Concern: The gradient h uses current `cur` but after accepted we don't recompute h; okay.

`soft_weights(b, p)`:
```
def soft_weights(b, p):
    bb=np.maximum(b,0)
    if p==1:
       s=bb.sum()
       return bb/s if s>0 else np.ones_like(bb)/len
    # To avoid overflow: logw = p*log(bb+eps)-max
    eps = max(1e-300, bb.max()*1e-30)
    logw = p*np.log(bb+eps)
    logw -= logw.max()
    w=np.exp(logw)
    s=w.sum()
    if s==0: return np.ones_like(bb)/len
    w/=s
    return w
```
If p large, only max weights; gradient similar to active.

`choose_active(b, top, frac)`:
```
def choose_active(b, top, frac):
    mb=b.max()
    thr=mb*frac
    idx=np.where(b>=thr)[0]
    if len(idx)==0: return np.array([int(np.argmax(b))])
    if len(idx)>top:
        # return top largest within threshold
        part=np.argpartition(b[idx], -top)[-top:]
        return idx[part]
    return idx
```
Use `top` can be > len.

Potential issue: In active_gradient, if `indices` unsorted, loop okay. For performance, unique indices. Maybe sort not needed.

`b2_greedy_build(n, limit, rng, passes=1)`:

st=b2_state(n,limit)
for p in range(passes):
   order=rng.permutation(n)
   added=0
   for x in order:
      if st['k'] == n: break
      if b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):

   if added==0 and p>0: break
return st
```
This could be slow if passes many; one pass enough.

`scores`: For binary state, `b2_binary_score(st)`:
```
def b2_binary_score(st):
   k=st['k']
   if k<=0: return inf
   maxc=int(st['c'].max())
   return float(2.0*st['n']*maxc/(k*k))
```

`b2_local_search(st, limit, time_budget, rng, target_k=None, verbose=False)`:
We'll maintain best. We need convert state to support for best. Since B2 state modifies arrays; best support copy saved.
Pseudo:
```
def b2_local_search(st, limit, time_budget, rng, verbose=False):
    T0=time.monotonic(); Tlim=T0+max(0,time_budget)-1
    best_score=inf; best_support=None
    original_support=st['support'].copy? no
    it=0
    while time < Tlim:
       k=st['k']
       if k>0:
          score=b2_binary_score(st)
          if score < best_score:
             best_score=score; best_support=st['sup'][:k].copy()
             if verbose and it%100: print
       maxc = int(st['c'].max())
       if maxc <= limit:
           # feasible under limit. Try add
           added = False
           # Up to 20 random feasible additions; more likely if many slots
           trials = 5 + (0 if k>200 else 20)
           for _ in range(trials):
              x = int(rng.integers(st['n']))
              if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
                 b2_add(st,x); added=True; break
           if not added:
              # perform a reshuffling swap
              if k == 0:
                 # add any? limit maybe; feasible
                 x=int(rng.integers(n)); b2_add(st,x)
              else:
                 # choose y from highest sum or random
                 s = int(np.argmax(st['c']))
                 y = b2_conflict_element(st, s, rng)
                 if y is None:
                    y = int(st['sup'][int(rng.integers(k))])
                 b2_remove(st,y)
                 # try to add until size restored or 50 fails
                 for _ in range(80):
                    x=int(rng.integers(n))
                    if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
                       b2_add(st,x)
                       if st['k'] >= k: break
       else:
           # reduce conflicts
           s=int(np.argmax(st['c']))
           y=b2_conflict_element(st,s,rng)
           if y is None:
              # pick element in highest count sums maybe random
              y=int(st['sup'][int(rng.integers(k))])
           b2_remove(st,y)
           # optionally immediately add to keep k
           # try several random feasible; if success, continue; if not, leave smaller
           for _ in range(30):
              x=int(rng.integers(n))
              if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
                 b2_add(st,x)
                 break
       it += 1
       # Occasionally if stuck (no improvement for many iterations), random reset part? Maybe just continue.
    if best_support is not None:
       return best_support, best_score
    # fallback current
    return st['sup'][:st['k']].copy(), b2_binary_score(st)
```
This may remove elements and never add if feasible rare, decreasing k. But best saved. ```

for x in range(n): if not in[x] and feasible_add: add; break
```

But `b2_feasible_add` for each x uses `np.max` over k array; for full scan 6121*200=1.2M numpy scalar ops? Actually each creates idx array length 200, overhead huge: 6121 calls * 200 =1.2M elements but 6121 Python calls to np.max; maybe 0.1s? Could be 1s. If done several times okay.

We can optimize feasible_add for full scan using convolution? For each x, check if c[x+S] has >limit-2. We can precompute for all x? For a fixed support S, compute positions that are forbidden: For each sum s with c[s] > limit-2, any x such that x = s - y for y∈S is forbidden. Mark forbidden bool. Also `bad2` where c[2x]>limit-1. This can be done O(k * high_counts) maybe but computing by loops over support and high sums? That's cross-correlation. Could compute by FFT or loop over support and high sums. But maybe not necessary.

For local search, random feasible likely good if many candidates. If stuck, full scan okay.

We need `b2_conflict_element` efficient. It scans support. Good.

Could implement a directed add for a random x that is infeasible: find conflicted y and swap directly. This may build faster.

Simpler: Use bounded deletion from noisy set and then target additions. Let's write a dedicated `b2_repair_random(n, L, start_k, rng, time)` that starts random set of start_k and deletes conflicts, then adds. It may be simpler than incremental.
```
def b2_repair_random(n, L, start_k, rng, time_chunk):
   st=empty; add start_k random distinct
   while time and c.max()>L:
      s=argmax; y=conflict; remove
   # now feasible; try add full scan until no add (with time limit)
   while time:
      if not add any (random/full): break
   return st
```
Then local swaps as above. This avoids toocomplicated state. We can call for several start_k and L. The deletion phase may remove many, leaving a subset. Let's implement this as core; then use local search to improve.

Add k random distinct: Use shuffle of permutation; take first k; add sequentially without checking (will exceed limit). Need support size maybe target+50. `st` counts after adding all random; count arrays update O(k^2) naive if add one by one with k up to 300 -> 45k updates, fine. But adding one by one with feasible not needed. After building, delete conflicts.

`b2_add_nocheck(st,x)` same as add. If x duplicate avoid.

Deletion loop: while c.max>L and st.k>0 and time<Tdel:
- s=argmax c
- y=conflict_element
- remove
This will reduce to a B2[L] subset. It may end with k maybe below target. Then addition phase:
- try to add random feasible; if no, full scan once; if no add, break.
This can recover.

After each phase, save best score.

This method is robust and conceptually simple. We'll use for L=3,4,5 with start_k around target+some. If the random set contains a large B2[L] subset, deletion will find it. If max size < target, final score not target but maybe okay.

Let's define:
```
def b2_random_repair(n, L, start_k, time_budget, rng):
   T0=time; Tdel=T0+time_budget*0.6; Tadd=T0+time_budget*0.85; Tloc=T0+time_budget-1
   st=b2_state(n,L)
   if start_k>n: start_k=n
   perm = rng.permutation(n)
   for x in perm[:start_k]:
       if not st['in'][x]: b2_add(st,x)
   # deletion
   while time < Tdel and st['k']>0:
       maxc=int(st['c'].max())
       if maxc <= L: break
       s=int(np.argmax(st['c']))
       y=b2_conflict_element(st,s,rng)
       if y is None: y=int(st['sup'][int(rng.integers(st['k']))])
       b2_remove(st,y)
   # addition
   while time < Tadd:
       # try add via random trials and full scan occasionally
       k=st['k']
       added=False
       trials = 50
       for _ in range(trials):
          x=int(rng.integers(n))
          if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,L):
             b2_add(st,x); added=True; break
       if not added:
          # full scan up to n maybe if not done in last few? Use limited full scan: iterate x from 0 to n
          full=False
          for x in range(n):
             if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,L):
                 b2_add(st,x); full=True; break
          if not full:
             # do a few swaps to try to enable additions
             # if maxc<=L, remove a random high and attempt add
             if st['k']>0:

```
def random_harmonic(n, rng):
   m = int(rng.integers(2,6)) # harmonics
   coeffs = rng.normal(0,1,m)
   phases = rng.uniform(0,2*math.pi,m)
   freqs = rng.uniform(0.2, 2;m)
   x = np.zeros(n)
   for j in range(m): x += rng.uniform(-1,1)*np.cos(2*math.pi*freqs[j]*np.arange(n)/n + phases[j])
   a = np.exp(x - x.mean()) # or max(0, 1 + 0.8*x std)
   a = np.maximum(a, 1e-8); a/=sum
   return a
```

`b2_state`:
```
def b2_state(n, limit):
    return {
       'n': int(n), 'limit': int(limit),
       'sup': np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32),
       'pos': np.full(n, -1, dtype=np.int32),
       'in': np.zeros(n, dtype=np.bool_),
       'c': np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=np.int32),
       'k': 0
    }
```
If n large (6121), fine.

p=st['pos'][x]
if p < 0 or p >= st['k'] or not st['in'][x]: return

# update c
if k:
   idx = st['sup'][:k] + np.int32(x)
   st['c'][idx] -= 2
st['c'][2*x] += 1
last=st['sup'][k-1]

if p != k-1:

else:

`b2_feasible_add`:
```
def b2_feasible_add(st, x, limit=None):
    if limit is None: limit=st['limit']
    x=int(x)
    if x < 0 or x >= st['n'] or st['in'][x]: return False
    c=st['c']; k=st['k']; L=int(limit); n=st['n']
    if L <=0: return False
    if c[2*x] > L-1: return False
    if k:
       if L <=1: return False
       idx=st['sup'][:k] + np.int32(x)
       # if any c[idx] > L-2
       if np.max(c[idx]) > L-2: return False
    return True
```
For L=1, no sums allowed except? If k=0, c[0]=0≤0, k=0 -> feasible; add first element gives c[0]=1? Wait L=1 ordered, first element count 1 at 0; feasible. Our condition if k and L<=1 false, so for second x, no sums allowed if c[x+y]≤-1; impossible. Good.

`b2_conflict_element`:
```
def b2_conflict_element(st, s, rng):
    k=st['k']; n=st['n']; sup=st['sup']; inb=st['in']
    if k==0: return None
    cands=[]
    i=0
    while i<k:
       y=int(sup[i]); z=s-y
       if 0 <= z < n and inb[z]:
           # include a representative to avoid duplicates: min(y,z)
           rep = y if y <= z else z
           cands.append(rep)
       i+=1
    if not cands:
       return None
    return int(cands[int(rng.integers(len(cands)))])
```
If many duplicates, cands length up to k; random. Could be okay. If sum s has many pairs, cands length ~pairs. Good.

`b2_find_add_full(st,L)` loops all x; use limited time? We'll implement with max trial parameter.
```
def b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, max_trials=200, full_scan=False):
    n=st['n']
    if full_scan:
       for x in range(n):
          if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
              b2_add(st,x); return True
       return False
    for _ in range(max_trials):
       x=int(rng.integers(n))
       if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
           b2_add(st,x); return True
    return False
```
Full scan could be expensive but called rarely.

`b2_random_repair` more concrete:
```
def b2_random_repair(n, limit, start_k, time_budget, rng):
    if time_budget <=0: return None, inf
    T0=time.monotonic(); Tdel=T0+max(0.5,time_budget)*0.45; Tadd=T0+max(0.5,time_budget)*0.8; Tloc=T0+max(0.5,time_budget)-1.0
    st=b2_state(n,limit)
    if start_k > n: start_k=n
    if start_k <=0: return None, inf
    perm = rng.permutation(n)
    # Add start_k distinct without checking. If start_k close n, counts large.
    added=0
    for x in perm:
       if added >= start_k: break
       if not st['in'][int(x)]:
          b2_add(st,int(x)); added+=1
    # delete until max <= limit
    while time.monotonic() < Tdel and st['k'] > 0:
       maxc=int(st['c'].max())
       if maxc <= limit: break
       s=int(np.argmax(st['c']))
       y=b2_conflict_element(st,s,rng)
       if y is None:
          y=int(st['sup'][:st['k']][int(rng.integers(st['k']))])
       b2_remove(st,y)
       # if time exceeded break
    # save
    best_support=st['sup'][:st['k']].copy(); best_score=b2_binary_score(st)
    # addition phase
    no_add_count=0
    full_scan_every=0; last_full=-100
    moves=0
    while time.monotonic() < Tadd:
       score=b2_binary_score(st); if score<best_score: update
       maxc=int(st['c'].max())
       if maxc > limit:
          s=argmax; y=conflict remove; if y none random remove; continue
       # feasible
       if b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, max_trials=20, full_scan=(moves % 200 > 190)):
           no_add_count=0
       else:
           no_add_count+=1
           if no_add_count >= 10 and st['k']>0:
               # swap: remove from high or random, then try add several
               s=int(np.argmax(st['c']))
               y=b2_conflict_element(st,s,rng)
               if y is None: y=int(st['sup'][:st['k']][int(rng.integers(st['k']))])
               b2_remove(st,y)
               # try to add 50 random, then full if fail
               added=False
               for _ in range(50):
                  if b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, max_trials=1): break
               if not b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, max_trials=0, full_scan=True)?
               no_add_count=0
       moves+=1
    # local shuffle time
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < Tloc:
       score=... update
       maxc=...
       if maxc > limit: delete conflict; continue
       if b2_find_any_add(... full_scan sometimes): continue
       # do swap
       if st['k']>0:
          s=argmax; y=conflict or random; b2_remove(st,y); try add random/full
       it+=1
    return best_support,best_score
```

Define helper `apply_floor_mask`.

```
def apply_floor_mask(a, fixed_mask, floor):
    if fixed_mask is None:
        return np.maximum(a, floor)
    a = a.copy()
    a[~fixed_mask] = 0.0
    if floor > 0:
        a[fixed_mask] = np.maximum(a[fixed_mask], floor)
    return a
```

   # Build log_update length n initialized 0
   sup = support.copy()

   logu = np.zeros(k)
   for idx_i, si in enumerate(sup):
       sidx = si + sup
       # b at sidx should be >0 for pairs? Some b may be zero if weights tiny; use eps
       ratio = target / np.maximum(b[sidx], 1e-30)

   logu -= logu.mean()? Scaling? Overall factor negligible after normalize but can stabilize.
   logu = np.clip(logu, -3, 3)

Let's write `make_random_base(n, rng)`:
```
def make_random_sequence(n, rng):
   a = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, n))
   # add linear/asymmetric trend
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   a *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5)*x)
   a = np.maximum(a,1e-12)
   a/=a.sum(); return a
```

Let's write each carefully.

`_fft_len`:
```
_FFT_LEN_CACHE = {}
def _fft_len(L):
    L=int(L)
    if L in _FFT_LEN_CACHE: return _FFT_LEN_CACHE[L]
    N=int(np.fft.next_fast_len(L))
    _FFT_LEN_CACHE[L]=N
    return N
```

`clean_sequence`:
```
def clean_sequence(x):
    if x is None: return None
    arr=np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size==0: return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)): arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return arr
```

`direct_score`:
```
def direct_score(a):
    a=clean_sequence(a)
    if a is None: return float('inf')
    n=a.size
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01: return float('inf')
    if n <=1:
       m=float(a[0]); return 2.0*n*(m*m)/(s*s) # if n=1 and a clipped
    if n <= MAX_DIRECT_N:
       b=np.convolve(a,a)
       mb=float(np.max(b))
    else:
       b=convolution_normalized(a)
       return float(2.0*n*float(np.max(b)))
    return float(2.0*n*mb/(s*s))
```
For n large fallback normalized; but we don't use. If a all zeros s=0. For n=1, direct conv formula: b[0]=a0²; 2*1*a0²/a0²=2 if a0>0. We can just use general: if n>0, b maybe; but for n=1 np.convolve fine. We'll simplify no special.
```
if n <= MAX: b=np.convolve(a,a); mb=max; return 2*n*mb/(s*s)
else: b=convolution_normalized(a); mb=max; return 2*n*mb # since sum normalized. 
```
For n up to 12000 conv 144M maybe okay once; maybe set 10000.

`convolution_normalized` zero if sum<=0.

`normalize_with_mask`:
```
def normalize_with_mask(a, fixed_mask, floor):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    if a.size==0: return np.ones(1)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    if fixed_mask is not None:
       a[~fixed_mask]=0.0
       if floor>0:
          a[fixed_mask]=np.maximum(a[fixed_mask], floor)
    else:
       if floor>0: a=np.maximum(a, floor)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
       if fixed_mask is not None and fixed_mask.any():
           a[fixed_mask]=1.0; a[~fixed_mask]=0.0; s=fixed_mask.sum()
       else:
           a.fill(1.0); s=a.size
       if s<=0: a[:] = 1.0; s=a.size
    a /= s
    if fixed_mask is not None:
       a[~fixed_mask]=0.0
       s2=float(a.sum())
       if s2>0: a/=s2
    return a
```

`apply_floor_mask` similar but not normalize? For update:
```
def apply_floor_mask(a, fixed_mask, floor):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
    if fixed_mask is not None:
       a[~fixed_mask]=0.0
       if floor>0: a[fixed_mask]=np.maximum(a[fixed_mask], floor)
    else:
       if floor>0: a=np.maximum(a, floor)
    return a
```

`score_array`:
```
def score_array(a):
    a=clean_sequence(a)
    if a is None: return inf
    b=convolution_normalized(a)
    return float(2.0*a.size*float(np.max(b)))
```
If clean returns None (all zeros) inf.

`soft_weights` as above.

`choose_active_indices`:
```
def choose_active_indices(b, top_count, frac):
    mb=float(np.max(b))
    if mb <=0: return np.array([0])
    thr=max(1e-300, mb*frac)
    idx=np.where(b >= thr)[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
        return np.array([int(np.argmax(b))])
    if idx.size > top_count:
        part=np.argpartition(b[idx], -top_count)[-top_count:]
        return idx[part]
    return idx
```
If top_count <1? set 1.

`active_gradient`:
```
def active_gradient(a, indices):
    n=a.size
    h=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in indices:
       k=int(k)
       lo=k-(n-1)
       if lo<0: lo=0
       hi=k if k<n else n-1
       if lo>hi: continue
       h[k-hi:k-lo+1] += a[lo:hi+1][::-1]
    h *= 2.0
    h=np.maximum(h,0.0)
    return h
```
Check slice length: `k-lo+1` inclusive. If lo=0, hi=k<n, slice from k-k? for i? Example n=5,k=3: lo=0 hi=3; h[0:4] += a[0:4][::-1] length4. i values k-j =3..0, correct.

`dense_gradient`:
```
def dense_gradient(a,P):
   n=a.size
   N=_fft_len(3*n-2)
   fa=np.fft.rfft(P,N)
   fr=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],N)
   hc=np.fft.irfft(fa*fr,N)
   h=2.0*hc[n-1:2*n-1]
   h=np.maximum(h,0.0)
   return h
```
For n=1, 3n-2=1; slice 0:1; P length1; r length1; conv length1; h=2*a0P0. good.

`make_random_sequence`:
```
def make_random_sequence(n, rng):
    a=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.5,n))
    trend=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    a *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.5)*trend)
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-12)
    s=a.sum(); return a/s
```
`make_harmonic_sequence`:
```
def make_harmonic_sequence(n, rng):
    m=int(rng.integers(2,6))
    x=np.zeros(n)
    idx=np.arange(n)
    for _ in range(m):
       freq=float(rng.uniform(0.05, 2.0))
       phase=float(rng.uniform(0, 2*math.pi))
       amp=float(rng.uniform(-0.8, 0.8))
       x += amp*np.cos(2.0*math.pi*freq*idx/float(n) + phase)
    std=float(np.std(x))
    if std > 1e-12: x=(x-x.mean())/std
    else: x=rng.normal(0,1,n)
    a=1.0 + 0.7*np.tanh(x)
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-10)
    s=a.sum(); return a/s
```
Maybe use lower spike? Fine.

`active_optimize` as above. Need pass `mask` optional. It returns `(best, best_score)`. Let's write robustly.
```
def active_optimize(a, time_budget, fixed_mask=None, seed=0, verbose=False):
    Tinit=0?
    a = normalize_with_mask(a, fixed_mask, 0.0)
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_array(a)
    if time_budget < 3.0: return best, best_score
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    T0=time.monotonic(); Tlimit=T0+max(0.0,time_budget)-1.0
    cur=a.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    it=0; last_best=0
    top_cycle=[120,100,80,60,50,40,30,20,15,10,8,6,5,4,3,2,2,3,5,8]
    p_cycle=[2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256,512]
    total_bud=max(1.0,time_budget)
    while time.monotonic() < Tlimit:
        if it > 0 and it - last_best > 400:
            cur=best.copy()
            sigma=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.4))
            cur *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma,size=cur.size))
            cur=apply_floor_mask(cur,fixed_mask,1e-12)
            s=float(cur.sum())
            if s>0: cur/=s
            cur_score=score_array(cur)
            last_best=it
        b=convolution_normalized(cur)
        mb=float(b.max())
        cur_score=float(2.0*cur.size*mb)
        if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best=cur.copy(); best_score=cur_score; last_best=it
            if verbose: print('  active it',it,'score',best_score)
        elapsed=(time.monotonic()-T0)/total_bud
        # If best is already very low and no improvement, reduce top.
        use_smooth = (it < 400) and (not fixed_mask or it < 200)
        h=None; hm=0.0
        if use_smooth:
            p=p_cycle[min(len(p_cycle)-1, it//50)]
            P=soft_weights(b,p)
            h=dense_gradient(cur,P)
            hm=float(h.max())
            if hm <= 1e-300:
                h=None
        if h is None or hm <= 1e-300:
            top=int(top_cycle[min(len(top_cycle)-1, it//40)])
            frac=0.98 if top <=5 else 0.92
            idx=choose_active_indices(b, max(1,min(top, b.size)), frac)
            h=active_gradient(cur, idx)
            hm=float(h.max())
        accepted=False
        if hm > 1e-300:
            if use_smooth:
                alphas=[0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02]
            else:
                top=int(top_cycle[min(len(top_cycle)-1, it//40)])
                if top <=3:
                    alphas=[1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05]
                else:
                    alphas=[0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.02]
            scale = hm
            for alpha in alphas:
                cand = cur * np.exp(-alpha*(h/scale))
                cand=apply_floor_mask(cand, fixed_mask, 1e-12)
                s=float(cand.sum())
                if s <=0 or not np.isfinite(s): continue
                cand /= s
                sc=score_array(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score - 1e-11:
                    cur=cand; cur_score=sc; accepted=True; break
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-11:
                    # maybe accept if much better than best
                    cur=cand; cur_score=sc; accepted=True; break
        if not accepted:
            sigma=float(rng.uniform(0.03, 0.35))
            cand = cur * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma,size=cur.size))
            cand=apply_floor_mask(cand, fixed_mask, 1e-12)
            s=float(cand.sum())
            if s>0 and np.isfinite(s):
                cand/=s; sc=score_array(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    therm=max(1e-5, 0.01*(1.0-min(1.0,elapsed))) * max(cur_score,1e-6)
                    accept=False
                    if sc <= cur_score + 1e-12:
                        accept=True
                    elif sc <= cur_score + therm:
                        try:
                            if math.exp(-(sc-cur_score)/therm) > rng.random(): accept=True
                        except OverflowError:
                            accept=False
                    if accept:
                        cur=cand; cur_score=sc
                    elif best_score < cur_score and rng.random() < 0.05:
                        cur=best.copy()
        it += 1
        if verbose and it % 250 == 0:
            print('    active iter', it, 'cur', cur_score, 'best', best_score, 'time', time.monotonic()-T0)
    if cur_score < best_score:
        best=cur.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    return best, best_score
```
Potential bug: In `if sc < best_score` accept when not < cur but < best; if `best_score < cur_score`, then sc < best implies sc < cur? Actually if cur_score > best_score, sc<best<cur, so first condition true. If cur_score <=best, sc<best implies sc<cur? not necessarily: cur=2, best=1, sc=1.5 => first false? sc<cur? 1.5<2 true. Okay. If cur=1.2,best=1.1,sc=1.15, first false, second false (not <best). Good. Condition second redundant; can remove. We'll only accept if `sc < cur_score` or anneal in noise. Simpler.

`active_optimize` may reset to best probability 0.05 after noise no accept; but if cur bad, best preserved.

Potential performance: `score_array(cand)` recomputes convolution; in alpha loop 5 times. If no improvement often, expensive. We can limit to 3 alphas by time. Maybe okay.

`score_array` uses clean_sequence (copy) for cand, but cand already clean. We can make a faster `score_normalized_array` that assumes finite nonnegative. But no need? We can create `score_normalized(a)` without clean? Need sum. Let's add:
```
def score_normalized(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel()
    s=a.sum(); if s<=0 return inf
    a=a/s
    b=convolution_normalized(a) # this will normalize again if sum1? It will divide by 1; okay. Could avoid double.
```
`convolution_normalized` divides by sum every call; if a sum1, okay. To save, implement `convolution_normalized_raw(a)` assuming exact sum1? We can keep simple.

But in active loop, `convolution_normalized(cur)` already normalizes; then `score_array(cand)` normalizes. Fine.

Potential `math.exp` of large positive? only negative.

Now B2 functions. Let's code.

```
def b2_state(n, limit): ...
```
If 2n-1 large maybe memory. n ≤8192, counts 16k int32 fine. If n=0? no.

`b2_add`: Ensure x in range. If x already in, return. Could in random repair adding with no check use if not in.

`b2_remove`: Use pos. Need if `p` stale? We maintain. Good.

`b2_find_any_add` can call feasible_add many times. If `st['k'] >= st['n']`, false. In feasible, if k==n, no unselected.
```
def b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, max_trials=1, full_scan=False):
    n=st['n']; k=st['k']
    if k >= n: return False
    if not full_scan:
       for _ in range(max_trials):
          x=int(rng.integers(n))
          if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit): b2_add(st,x); return True
       return False
    for x in range(n):
       if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit): b2_add(st,x); return True
    return False
```
Full scan over Python; could be slow but okay rare. To avoid `b2_feasible_add` creating idx each x for k=0? if k=0, can just add first unselected. Add optimization:
```
if k==0:
   for x in range(n): b2_add(st,x); return True
```
In random, if k=0, random x can add. We can handle.

`b2_binary_score` returns inf if k=0.

`b2_random_repair` detail:
```
def b2_random_repair(n, limit, start_k, time_budget, rng):
    if time_budget <=0: return None, inf
    T0=time.monotonic(); Tlimit=T0+max(0,time_budget)-0.5
    st=b2_state(n,limit)
    start_k=int(start_k); if start_k<1: return None,inf
    if start_k>n: start_k=n
    perm=rng.permutation(n)
    added=0
    for val in perm:
       x=int(val)
       if not st['in'][x]:
          b2_add(st,x); added+=1
          if added>=start_k: break
    best_support=None; best_score=b2_binary_score(st)
    if st['k']>0: best_support=st['sup'][:st['k']].copy()
    no_add=0; full_scan_flags=[]
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < Tlimit:
       k=st['k']
       score=b2_binary_score(st)
       if k>0 and score < best_score:
           best_score=score; best_support=st['sup'][:k].copy()
       if k==0:
          x=int(rng.integers(n)); b2_add(st,x); no_add=0; it+=1; continue
       maxc=int(st['c'].max())
       if maxc > limit:
          s=int(np.argmax(st['c']))
          y=b2_conflict_element(st,s,rng)
          if y is None:
             y=int(st['sup'][int(rng.integers(k))])
          b2_remove(st,y)
          # sometimes try to add to keep size
          if b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, 5):
              no_add=0
          it+=1
          continue
       # feasible under limit
       # try add
       did_add=False
       if b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, 15):
           did_add=True; no_add=0
       else:
           no_add += 1
           if no_add >= 8 and k > 0:
               no_add=0
               old_k = st['k']
               s=int(np.argmax(st['c']))
               y=b2_conflict_element(st,s,rng)
               if y is None:
                  y=int(st['sup'][int(rng.integers(k))])
               if y is not None:
                  b2_remove(st,y)
                  # Try to add maybe until size restored or a few attempts
                  restored=False
                  for _ in range(30):
                      if b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, 5):
                          if st['k'] >= old_k:
                              restored=True; break
                  if not restored and not st['in'][y]:
                      b2_add(st,y)
       it +=1
       if it % 500 ==0: maybe print? no inside.
    # final update
    k=st['k']; score=b2_binary_score(st)
    if k>0 and score < best_score: best_support=st['sup'][:k].copy(); best_score=score
    return best_support, best_score
```
This may loop quickly; `b2_find_any_add` full_scan not used, only random. It might get stuck swapping same. But restarts help. We should enable full scan sometimes: In feasible no add, if no_add>=20, use full_scan once:
```
if no_add >= 20:
   no_add=0
   if b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, 0, full_scan=True): did_add=True
```
But full scan expensive; set no_add threshold 25 and maybe only if `time.monotonic()+0.2 < Tlimit`. Add.

```

else:
 no_add +=1
 if no_add >= 30:
   no_add=0
   if not b2_find_any_add(...,0,True):
      # swap
      ...
 elif no_add >= 8:
   # swap
```
Okay.

In maxc>limit conflict, after remove we can try add once with random no full.

This `b2_random_repair` may spend time in tight loops with no time quantification; but moves cheap. Good.

`b2_search`:
```
def b2_search(n, limit, time_budget, rng):
   if time_budget < 15: return None, inf
   T0=time.monotonic(); Tlimit=T0+max(0,time_budget)-2
   best_support=None; best_score=inf
   base_start = int(math.sqrt(n) * (1.5 + 0.35*limit))
   base_start = max(30, min(n, base_start))
   sizes=[]
   for factor in (0.8,1.0,1.2,1.5):
       s=int(base_start*factor); if s not in sizes: sizes.append(max(20,min(n,s)))
   r=0
   while time.monotonic() + 10 < Tlimit:
       ss = sizes[r % len(sizes)]
       # jitter
       ss = int(ss * (0.9 + 0.25*rng.random()))
       ss = max(20, min(n, ss))
       # allocate chunk: maybe 20-40s
       chunk=min(60.0, Tlimit-time.monotonic())
       sup,score=b2_random_repair(n,limit,ss,chunk,rng)
       if sup is not None and score < best_score:
           best_support=sup.copy(); best_score=score
       r += 1
       # If score < required? continue anyway
   return best_support,best_score
```
If chunk min 60 but remaining 30, okay; loop condition ensures +10. Could call one at a time; total time. Good.

Maybe multiple start sizes in same chunk? okay.

`balance_weights` function:
```
def balance_weights(support, n, time_budget, rng=None, verbose=False):
    support=np.asarray(support,int).ravel(); support=support[(support>=0)&(support<n)]
    if support.size <3 or time_budget<5: return None,inf
    # unique
    support=np.unique(support)
    k=support.size
    if k > 1500: return None,inf
    a=np.zeros(n); a[support]=1.0/k; a=s/a.sum()
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score_array(a)
    cur=a.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    # precompute counts binary for flat target
    counts=np.zeros(2*n-1,dtype=np.int32)
    for x in support:
       counts[support + int(x)] += 1
    active_counts = counts > 0
    mach=int(np.count_nonzero(active_counts))
    flat=np.zeros(2*n-1); flat[active_counts]=1.0/mach
    if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(1)
    T0=time.monotonic(); Tlimit=T0+max(0,time_budget)-1
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()<Tlimit:
       b=convolution_normalized(cur)
       cur_score=float(2*n*float(b.max()))
       if cur_score < best_score -1e-12: best=cur.copy(); best_score=cur_score
       # flatten fraction increasing
       frac = min(1.0, 0.3 + 0.7*it/500.0)
       target = (1.0-frac)*b + frac*flat
       ts=target.sum()
       if ts>0: target=target/ts
       # log update for support
       logu=np.zeros(k, dtype=np.float64)
       for i, si in enumerate(support):
           sidx = support + int(si)
           bval = b[sidx]
           # target[sidx] should >0 for active; if not, 0. ratio at active.
           ratio = target[sidx] / np.maximum(bval, 1e-30)
           # Clip ratio to [1e-6,1e6]
           lr = np.log(np.clip(ratio, 1e-8, 1e8))
           logu[i] = 0.5 * float(np.sum(lr))
       logu -= float(np.mean(logu))
       logu = np.clip(logu, -2.0, 2.0)
       accepted=False
       for tau in (0.75, 0.5, 0.25, 0.1):
           w = cur[support] * np.exp(tau*logu)
           w = np.maximum(w, 1e-12)
           ws=float(w.sum())
           if ws<=0: continue
           wd=w/ws
           cand=cur.copy()
           cand[support]=wd
           sc=score_array(cand)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score - 1e-11:
               cur=cand; cur_score=sc; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
           # random perturb weights
           w=cur[support]
           w *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.1,w.size))
           w=np.maximum(w,1e-12); w/=w.sum()
           cur[support]=w
       it+=1
       if verbose and it%200==0: print('  balance iter', it, best_score)
    if cur_score<best_score: best=cur.copy(); best_score=cur_score
    return best,best_score
```
The nested loop over support length k and vector length k; support is int array; `support + int(si)` creates new array. Good. If k=300, 300*300=90k operations per iter. 200 iter=18M, okay. But Python loop 300 per iter plus numpy; maybe okay.

Can optimize by precomputing? Not needed.

Potential bug: `candidate` copy each tau; expensive but okay. Could create zeros and set. We use `cur.copy()` length n 6121 per candidate; for 4* maybe. Fine. We can optimize by `cand=np.zeros(n); cand[support]=wd`; but support list. Use zero.

Could update curvature: If `cur` sum1, `cand` sum1. Good.

Now, `propose_candidate` code. Need use global height_sequence_1. Let's write exactly.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    budget=float(budget_s)
    if budget <=0: return [1.0]
    deadline=t_start + max(0.0, budget-8.0)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if ... else 42)
    # determine n
    n = DEFAULT_N
    base_arr=None
    try:
       raw = height_sequence_1
    except NameError:
       raw = None

```
def support_binary_score(support,n):
    s=np.asarray(support,int).ravel(); s=s[(s>=0)&(s<n)]; k=s.size
    if k==0: return inf
    counts=np.zeros(2*n-1,dtype=np.int32)
    for x in s:
        counts[s + int(x)] += 1
    return float(2*n*int(counts.max())/(k*k))
```

Let's write `record_best`:
```
def record_best(store, arr, score):
    if arr is None or not np.isfinite(score): return
    arr=clean_sequence(arr)
    if arr is None: return
    score=float(score)
    if score < store.get('score', float('inf')):
        store['score']=score
        store['seq']=arr.copy()
    cands=store.get('cands')
    if cands is None:
        cands=[]; store['cands']=cands
    # To limit, if cands len < 12 or score < max score in cands
    if len(cands) < 12:
        cands.append((score, arr.copy()))
    else:
        max_idx=0; max_val=cands[0][0]
        for i in range(len(cands)):
            if cands[i][0] > max_val:
                max_val=cands[i][0]; max_idx=i
        if score < max_val:
            cands[max_idx]=(score, arr.copy())
```
O(12) per record; records few.

def final_select(store):
    if store.get('seq') is None: return np.ones(DEFAULT_N,float)
    seqs=[store['seq']]
    for _,arr in store.get('cands',[]):
       seqs.append(arr)

    # avoid duplicates by simple tuple of first/last? Could skip.

       sc=direct_score(s)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
           best=s; best_sc=sc
    if best is None or not np.isfinite(best_sc): best=store['seq']
    return best, best_sc

def score_normalized_array(a):

def b2_find_any_add(st, limit, rng, max_trials=1, full_scan=False):
    n=st['n']; k=st['k']
    if k >= n: return False
    if k == 0:
       if full_scan:
          b2_add(st, 0); return True
       x=int(rng.integers(n)); b2_add(st,x); return True
    if not full_scan:
       for _ in range(max_trials):
          x=int(rng.integers(n))
          if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
             b2_add(st,x); return True
       return False
    # Full scan but sample first random? To avoid overly long, scan all.
    for x in range(n):
       if not st['in'][x] and b2_feasible_add(st,x,limit):
          b2_add(st,x); return True
    return False
```
